    "oldString": "% exp_ids: phase2_docred_grammar_only\n\\paragraph{Grammar layer surfaces latent violations.}\nGrammar-only constraints eliminate RV and ET but \\emph{increase} DR to 26.17\\% ($+$42\\%): the baseline's 29.66\\% OOV triples cannot be evaluated for DR; grammar enforcement assigns valid relation names whose domain/range rules the associated types frequently violate, \\emph{revealing} latent incompatibilities that motivate the semantic layer.\n\n% exp_ids: phase2_docred_semantic_only, phase2_docred_full_s1\n\\paragraph{Enabling dependency and tightness ceiling.}\nSemantic-only reduces DR by 25\\% but cannot eliminate RV (29.87\\%) or prevent ET increases (2.48\\%\\,$\\to$\\,4.22\\%), which replicates SciERC's enabling dependency.\nResidual DR (10.21\\%) exceeds SciERC's (0.93\\%) due to higher constraint tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$ vs.\\ 0.333): residual DR of 10.21\\% implies effective semantic coverage of approximately 90\\%, consistent with tight constraints forcing frequent grammar-first fallback (Appendix~\\ref{app:fallback-coverage}).\nUnder fine-tuning, this residual narrows to 0.01--0.20\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}): tightness-driven fallback is largely resolved once model calibration improves.",
    "newString": "% exp_ids: phase2_docred_grammar_only, phase2_docred_semantic_only, phase2_docred_full_s1\n\\paragraph{Layer contributions.}\nGrammar-only eliminates RV and ET but \\emph{increases} DR to 26.17\\%: OOV triples (29.66\\% baseline) become evaluable, \\emph{revealing} latent type incompatibilities.\nSemantic-only reduces DR by 25\\% but cannot eliminate RV or ET, replicating SciERC's enabling dependency.\nResidual DR (10.21\\% vs.\\ SciERC's 0.93\\%) reflects higher tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$ vs.\\ 0.333) forcing frequent fallback (Appendix~\\ref{app:fallback-coverage}); under fine-tuning, residual narrows to 0.01--0.20\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}).",
    "originalFile": "% Section 5 — DocRED Results (compressed, 1450→~600 words)\n% exp_ids: phase2_docred_unconstrained, phase2_docred_grammar_only,\n%          phase2_docred_semantic_only, phase2_docred_full_s1\n% All numbers verified against registry 2026-05-14.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Dataset Validation on DocRED}\n\\label{sec:docred-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:docred-ablation} presents the four-way ablation on DocRED (96 Wikidata relation types, 14$\\times$ SciERC; Qwen3-8B, zero-shot, 998 documents).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: DocRED four-way ablation\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{5}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-6} \\cmidrule(l){7-8}\n\\textbf{Mode}\n  & DR\\,$\\downarrow$ & $\\Delta$DR\n  & RV\\,$\\downarrow$ & ET\\,$\\downarrow$ & CF\n  & Rel.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nUnconstrained\n  & 18.46 & ---\n  & 29.66 & 2.48\n  & 0.10\n  & 7.74  & 58.15 \\\\\nGrammar\n  & 26.17 & $\\uparrow$42\\%\n  & \\textbf{0.03} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.07\n  & \\textbf{8.31}  & \\textbf{59.38} \\\\\nSemantic\n  & 13.84 & $\\downarrow$25\\%\n  & 29.87 & 4.22\n  & 1.05\n  & 7.70  & 57.81 \\\\\nFull (S1)\n  & \\textbf{10.21} & $\\downarrow$\\textbf{45\\%}\n  & \\textbf{0.03} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 2.57\n  & 8.23  & 59.37 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Four-way ablation on DocRED (998 documents, Qwen3-8B, zero-shot).\n$\\Delta$DR: relative change vs.\\ unconstrained.\nBest per column in \\textbf{bold} (CF excluded).\nF1 $\\times$100. Bootstrap significance: R-F1 $+$0.50\\,pp ($p < 0.001$).}\n\\label{tab:docred-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\n% exp_ids: phase2_docred_unconstrained, phase2_docred_full_s1\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}\nFull S1 reduces DR from 18.46\\% to 10.21\\% ($\\downarrow$45\\%), eliminates ET ($\\to$\\,0\\%), and near-eliminates RV (29.66\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.03\\%).\\footnote{The residual 0.03\\% RV traces to a rare XGrammar automaton edge case where subword tokens from different enum entries combine into chimeric values (e.g., \\texttt{member} + \\texttt{icipant\\_of} from \\texttt{member\\_of} and \\texttt{participant\\_of}). This is not a fundamental limitation of the approach.}\nR-F1 improves from 7.74 to 8.23 ($+$0.50\\,pp; $p < 0.001$), attributable to elimination of 5{,}189 OOV relation predictions.\nCF increases modestly (0.10\\%\\,$\\to$\\,2.57\\%), consistent with the CF$\\leftrightarrow$DR trade-off (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\n% exp_ids: phase2_docred_grammar_only\n\\paragraph{Grammar layer surfaces latent violations.}\nGrammar-only constraints eliminate RV and ET but \\emph{increase} DR to 26.17\\% ($+$42\\%): the baseline's 29.66\\% OOV triples cannot be evaluated for DR; grammar enforcement assigns valid relation names whose domain/range rules the associated types frequently violate, \\emph{revealing} latent incompatibilities that motivate the semantic layer.\n\n% exp_ids: phase2_docred_semantic_only, phase2_docred_full_s1\n\\paragraph{Enabling dependency and tightness ceiling.}\nSemantic-only reduces DR by 25\\% but cannot eliminate RV (29.87\\%) or prevent ET increases (2.48\\%\\,$\\to$\\,4.22\\%), which replicates SciERC's enabling dependency.\nResidual DR (10.21\\%) exceeds SciERC's (0.93\\%) due to higher constraint tightness ($\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$ vs.\\ 0.333): residual DR of 10.21\\% implies effective semantic coverage of approximately 90\\%, consistent with tight constraints forcing frequent grammar-first fallback (Appendix~\\ref{app:fallback-coverage}).\nUnder fine-tuning, this residual narrows to 0.01--0.20\\% (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}): tightness-driven fallback is largely resolved once model calibration improves.\n",

\\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Mistral\\\\7B}}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 37.3 & 63.9 & 0 & 0 & 2.4 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 64.0 & 13.8 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1 not applicable (grammar-only has no semantic-layer metrics).\nF1 $\\times$ 100.}\n\\label{tab:wikievents}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Results.}\nUnconstrained generation produces extreme vocabulary violations (EV+RoV exceeding 85\\% combined; Table~\\ref{tab:wikievents}), and all models yield entity F1\\,=\\,0 because the parser cannot identify well-formed mentions in off-schema output.\nFull S1 eliminates EV and RoV across all three models ($\\to$\\,0\\%), which validates tokenizer-agnostic design across byte-level BPE (Qwen, Llama) and SentencePiece (Mistral).\nGrammar-only ablation isolates each layer: grammar eliminates EV ($\\to$\\,0\\%) but leaves RoV at 64--77\\% because the CFG cannot constrain 80 argument roles conditioned on event context; AC rises from near-zero to 8--14\\%, the same latent-violation surfacing pattern observed in DocRED (\\S\\ref{sec:docred-results}).\nFull S1 then drives RoV to 0\\%, which demonstrates layer complementarity.\nResidual AC (1.3--3.8\\%) traces to confusion between semantically similar entity types (e.g., SID vs.\\ SEN) where grammar-first fallback permits the incorrect type.\nCF rises to 15--21\\%, exceeding RE levels (10--12\\%) because EE's multi-argument event structures increase entity reuse across slots: a single entity may fill roles in multiple events, each imposing different type constraints through per-slot enforcement.\n\nPost-hoc filtering is impractical for EE: it retains only 23/5{,}467 arguments for Qwen (99.6\\% discarded), 3/5{,}053 for Llama, and 0/6{,}034 for Mistral, because unconstrained models generate generic role names rather than ontology-specific ones.\nUnlike RE, where constraints are F1-neutral, EE shows measurable gains: entity F1 rises from 0\\% to 11.7--17.6\\% and trigger F1 increases by $+$1.5--1.8\\,pp, because grammar enforcement produces schema-valid JSON that the parser can process.\n\n% exp_ids: p1_ee_ft_qwen_wikievents_r5, p1_ee_ft_llama_wikievents_r5\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\nLoRA fine-tuning extends composability to EE (20-document test set).\nUnconstrained FT models retain substantial RoV (Qwen 38.31\\%, Llama 8.8\\%) and AC violations (2.68\\%, 3.5\\%); adding S1 eliminates RoV for both models and reduces AC to $\\leq$\\,0.8\\% (Qwen 0\\%, Llama 0.8\\%).\nArgument F1 remains within $\\pm$1.1\\,pp (Qwen 7.85$\\to$8.18, Llama 9.98$\\to$8.91); trigger F1 shows a larger shift ($-$5.4\\,pp Qwen, $-$0.6\\,pp Llama), though both metrics carry wide confidence intervals at this sample size.\nThe violation-elimination pattern replicates RE composability (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}): FT reduces but does not eliminate schema violations; S1 removes residual RoV and AC.\n",

    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Results.}\nUnconstrained generation produces extreme vocabulary violations (EV+RoV exceeding 85\\% combined; Table~\\ref{tab:wikievents}), and all models yield entity F1\\,=\\,0 because the parser cannot identify well-formed mentions in off-schema output.\nFull S1 eliminates EV and RoV across all three models ($\\to$\\,0\\%), which validates tokenizer-agnostic design across byte-level BPE (Qwen, Llama) and SentencePiece (Mistral).\nGrammar-only ablation isolates each layer: grammar eliminates EV ($\\to$\\,0\\%) but leaves RoV at 64--77\\% because the CFG cannot constrain 80 argument roles conditioned on event context; AC rises from near-zero to 8--14\\%, the same latent-violation surfacing pattern observed in DocRED (\\S\\ref{sec:docred-results}).\nFull S1 then drives RoV to 0\\%, which demonstrates layer complementarity.\nResidual AC (1.3--3.8\\%) traces to confusion between semantically similar entity types (e.g., SID vs.\\ SEN) where grammar-first fallback permits the incorrect type.\nCF rises to 15--21\\%, exceeding RE levels (10--12\\%) because EE's multi-argument event structures increase entity reuse across slots: a single entity may fill roles in multiple events, each imposing different type constraints through per-slot enforcement.\n\nPost-hoc filtering is impractical for EE: it retains only 23/5{,}467 arguments for Qwen (99.6\\% discarded), 3/5{,}053 for Llama, and 0/6{,}034 for Mistral, because unconstrained models generate generic role names rather than ontology-specific ones.\nUnlike RE, where constraints are F1-neutral, EE shows measurable gains: entity F1 rises from 0\\% to 11.7--17.6\\% and trigger F1 increases by $+$1.5--1.8\\,pp, because grammar enforcement produces schema-valid JSON that the parser can process.",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Results.}\nUnconstrained EE produces extreme vocabulary violations (EV+RoV $>$85\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:wikievents}), with entity F1\\,=\\,0 because the parser cannot process off-schema output.\nFull S1 eliminates EV and RoV across all models, validating tokenizer-agnostic design across byte-level BPE and SentencePiece.\nGrammar-only eliminates EV but leaves RoV at 64--77\\% and surfaces latent AC (8--14\\%), replicating DocRED's pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:docred-results}); full S1 drives RoV to 0\\%.\nResidual AC (1.3--3.8\\%) traces to semantically similar types (e.g., SID vs.\\ SEN).\nCF rises to 15--21\\%, exceeding RE levels (10--12\\%) because EE's multi-argument structures increase entity reuse across slots.\nPost-hoc filtering is impractical ($<$0.5\\% arguments retained).\nUnlike RE, EE shows measurable F1 gains: entity F1 0\\%\\,$\\to$\\,12--18\\%, trigger F1 $+$1.5--1.8\\,pp.",
    "originalFile": "% Section 5 — Event Extraction on WikiEvents (compact, inline)\n% exp_ids: ee_qwen_wikievents_unconstrained, ee_llama_wikievents_unconstrained,\n%          ee_mistral_wikievents_unconstrained, ee_qwen_wikievents_constrained,\n%          ee_llama_wikievents_constrained, ee_mistral_wikievents_constrained_v2\n% v3 final data confirmed by Director 2026-05-18.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Task Validation: Event Extraction}\n\\label{sec:wikievents}\n\nWe apply OntoCoDec to event extraction (EE) on WikiEvents~\\cite{li2021document} (56 event types, 80 roles, 27 entity types, 245 constraints; Appendix~\\ref{app:wikievents}) without modification.\nViolations extend to EV (event vocabulary), RoV (role vocabulary), and AC (argument constraint, analogous to DR).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: WikiEvents three-model comparison (v3 final)\n%  exp_ids: ee_{qwen,llama,mistral}_wikievents_{unconstrained,grammar_only,constrained_v3}\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{2.8pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{4}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-6} \\cmidrule(l){7-8}\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & EV\\,$\\downarrow$ & RoV\\,$\\downarrow$ & AC & CF\n  & Trig.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 24.0 & 74.9 & 0.3 & 0 & 11.8 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 76.1 & 8.2 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 2.1 & 21.1 & 13.4 & 14.1 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 10.5 & 88.8 & 0.1 & 0 & 5.7 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 77.0 & 8.8 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 3.8 & 17.2 & 7.5 & 17.6 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Mistral\\\\7B}}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 37.3 & 63.9 & 0 & 0 & 2.4 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 64.0 & 13.8 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 1.3 & 14.9 & 3.9 & 11.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{WikiEvents EE results (246 documents, zero-shot).\nViolation rates over parsed documents.\n---: not applicable (grammar-only has no semantic-layer metrics).\nF1 $\\times$ 100.}\n\\label{tab:wikievents}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Results.}\nUnconstrained generation produces extreme vocabulary violations (EV+RoV exceeding 85\\% combined; Table~\\ref{tab:wikievents}), and all models yield entity F1\\,=\\,0 because the parser cannot identify well-formed mentions in off-schema output.\nFull S1 eliminates EV and RoV across all three models ($\\to$\\,0\\%), which validates tokenizer-agnostic design across byte-level BPE (Qwen, Llama) and SentencePiece (Mistral).\nGrammar-only ablation isolates each layer: grammar eliminates EV ($\\to$\\,0\\%) but leaves RoV at 64--77\\% because the CFG cannot constrain 80 argument roles conditioned on event context; AC rises from near-zero to 8--14\\%, the same latent-violation surfacing pattern observed in DocRED (\\S\\ref{sec:docred-results}).\nFull S1 then drives RoV to 0\\%, which demonstrates layer complementarity.\nResidual AC (1.3--3.8\\%) traces to confusion between semantically similar entity types (e.g., SID vs.\\ SEN) where grammar-first fallback permits the incorrect type.\nCF rises to 15--21\\%, exceeding RE levels (10--12\\%) because EE's multi-argument event structures increase entity reuse across slots: a single entity may fill roles in multiple events, each imposing different type constraints through per-slot enforcement.\n\nPost-hoc filtering is impractical for EE: it retains only 23/5{,}467 arguments for Qwen (99.6\\% discarded), 3/5{,}053 for Llama, and 0/6{,}034 for Mistral, because unconstrained models generate generic role names rather than ontology-specific ones.\nUnlike RE, where constraints are F1-neutral, EE shows measurable gains: entity F1 rises from 0\\% to 11.7--17.6\\% and trigger F1 increases by $+$1.5--1.8\\,pp, because grammar enforcement produces schema-valid JSON that the parser can process.\n\n% exp_ids: p1_ee_ft_qwen_wikievents_r5, p1_ee_ft_llama_wikievents_r5\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\nLoRA fine-tuning extends composability to EE (20-document test set).\nUnconstrained FT models retain substantial RoV (Qwen 38.31\\%, Llama 8.8\\%) and AC violations (2.68\\%, 3.5\\%); adding S1 eliminates RoV for both models and reduces AC to $\\leq$\\,0.8\\% (Qwen 0\\%, Llama 0.8\\%).\nArgument F1 remains within $\\pm$1.1\\,pp (Qwen 7.85$\\to$8.18, Llama 9.98$\\to$8.91); trigger F1 shows a larger shift ($-$5.4\\,pp Qwen, $-$0.6\\,pp Llama), though both metrics carry wide confidence intervals at this sample size.\nThe violation-elimination pattern replicates RE composability (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}): FT reduces but does not eliminate schema violations; S1 removes residual RoV and AC.\n",

    "originalFile": "% Section 5 — Event Extraction on WikiEvents (compact, inline)\n% exp_ids: ee_qwen_wikievents_unconstrained, ee_llama_wikievents_unconstrained,\n%          ee_mistral_wikievents_unconstrained, ee_qwen_wikievents_constrained,\n%          ee_llama_wikievents_constrained, ee_mistral_wikievents_constrained_v2\n% v3 final data confirmed by Director 2026-05-18.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Task Validation: Event Extraction}\n\\label{sec:wikievents}\n\nWe apply OntoCoDec to event extraction (EE) on WikiEvents~\\cite{li2021document} (56 event types, 80 roles, 27 entity types, 245 constraints; Appendix~\\ref{app:wikievents}) without modification.\nViolations extend to EV (event vocabulary), RoV (role vocabulary), and AC (argument constraint, analogous to DR).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: WikiEvents three-model comparison (v3 final)\n%  exp_ids: ee_{qwen,llama,mistral}_wikievents_{unconstrained,grammar_only,constrained_v3}\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{2.8pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& & \\multicolumn{4}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){3-6} \\cmidrule(l){7-8}\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode}\n  & EV\\,$\\downarrow$ & RoV\\,$\\downarrow$ & AC & CF\n  & Trig.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{Qwen3-8B}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 24.0 & 74.9 & 0.3 & 0 & 11.8 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 76.1 & 8.2 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 2.1 & 21.1 & 13.4 & 14.1 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Llama\\\\3.1-8B}}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 10.5 & 88.8 & 0.1 & 0 & 5.7 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 77.0 & 8.8 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 3.8 & 17.2 & 7.5 & 17.6 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{\\shortstack[l]{Mistral\\\\7B}}\n  & Unconstr.\\ & 37.3 & 63.9 & 0 & 0 & 2.4 & 0 \\\\\n  & Grammar    & \\textbf{0} & 64.0 & 13.8 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n  & Full S1    & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & 1.3 & 14.9 & 3.9 & 11.7 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{WikiEvents EE results (246 documents, zero-shot).\nViolation rates over parsed documents.\n---: not applicable (grammar-only has no semantic-layer metrics).\nF1 $\\times$ 100.}\n\\label{tab:wikievents}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Results.}\nUnconstrained EE produces extreme vocabulary violations (EV+RoV $>$85\\%; Table~\\ref{tab:wikievents}), with entity F1\\,=\\,0 because the parser cannot process off-schema output.\nFull S1 eliminates EV and RoV across all models, validating tokenizer-agnostic design across byte-level BPE and SentencePiece.\nGrammar-only eliminates EV but leaves RoV at 64--77\\% and surfaces latent AC (8--14\\%), replicating DocRED's pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:docred-results}); full S1 drives RoV to 0\\%.\nResidual AC (1.3--3.8\\%) traces to semantically similar types (e.g., SID vs.\\ SEN).\nCF rises to 15--21\\%, exceeding RE levels (10--12\\%) because EE's multi-argument structures increase entity reuse across slots.\nPost-hoc filtering is impractical ($<$0.5\\% arguments retained).\nUnlike RE, EE shows measurable F1 gains: entity F1 0\\%\\,$\\to$\\,12--18\\%, trigger F1 $+$1.5--1.8\\,pp.\n\n% exp_ids: p1_ee_ft_qwen_wikievents_r5, p1_ee_ft_llama_wikievents_r5\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\nLoRA fine-tuning extends composability to EE (20-document test set).\nUnconstrained FT models retain substantial RoV (Qwen 38.31\\%, Llama 8.8\\%) and AC violations (2.68\\%, 3.5\\%); adding S1 eliminates RoV for both models and reduces AC to $\\leq$\\,0.8\\% (Qwen 0\\%, Llama 0.8\\%).\nArgument F1 remains within $\\pm$1.1\\,pp (Qwen 7.85$\\to$8.18, Llama 9.98$\\to$8.91); trigger F1 shows a larger shift ($-$5.4\\,pp Qwen, $-$0.6\\,pp Llama), though both metrics carry wide confidence intervals at this sample size.\nThe violation-elimination pattern replicates RE composability (\\S\\ref{sec:composability}): FT reduces but does not eliminate schema violations; S1 removes residual RoV and AC.\n",

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nFull S1 overhead varies with document length: $+$13.1\\% on SciERC (short documents) and $+$2.43\\% on DocRED (long documents) under zero-shot greedy decoding, as the fixed per-token constraint cost is amortized over longer generation sequences (Qwen3-8B, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell).\nUnder LoRA fine-tuning, overhead is $+$10.0\\% on SciERC, with absolute latency 37\\% lower than zero-shot due to shorter, more structured outputs.\nGrammar compile is a one-time 44\\,ms cost.\nDetails in Appendix~\\ref{app:runtime}.\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nCombined with LoRA fine-tuning, beam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all residual violations: both Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B achieve zero violations across all four metrics (ET, RV, DR, CF) on SciERC, with R-F1 of 44.23 and 46.11 respectively.\nUnder zero-shot decoding, beam search resolves greedy's two residual limitations on SciERC (551 documents): DR drops to 0\\% for calibrated models (Qwen 0.93\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%; Llama 0.08\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0\\%) and CF decreases substantially (Qwen 9.90\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.53\\%; Llama 6.56\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.93\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\n\n% exp_ids: 70b_scierc_unconstrained, 70b_scierc_constrained, 70b_docred_unconstrained, 70b_docred_constrained\n\\paragraph{Cross-scale generalizability.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nTo evaluate cross-scale generalizability, we test OntoCoDec on Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct (AWQ INT4 quantization).\nOn SciERC, the 70B model produces fewer baseline violations than its 8B counterpart (DR 6.5\\% vs.\\ 13.3\\%), yet violations persist; OntoCoDec eliminates them entirely (DR$\\to$0\\%, R-F1 $-$0.03\\,pp).\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), the 70B model still exhibits substantial violations (RV\\,=\\,7.81\\%, DR\\,=\\,14.99\\%) despite lower rates than 8B models; OntoCoDec achieves \\textbf{0\\%} across ET, RV, and DR on the full 998-document set with no F1 loss (R-F1 13.07 vs.\\ 13.03, $+$0.04\\,pp), the only configuration to completely eliminate DR on DocRED.\nCF rises to 7.01\\% (from 0.07\\%); the low unconstrained baseline reflects under-measurement (malformed outputs bypass the checker) rather than genuine type consistency, while constrained decoding improves output validity and exposes more triples to the consistency check, consistent with the per-slot enforcement trade-off at 8B scale (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\nThe pattern is consistent: model scale reduces but does not eliminate ontology violations, and constraint enforcement remains necessary even at 70B scale.\n\nAppendix~\\ref{app:case-studies} presents representative case studies illustrating constraint behavior across violation types.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Runtime overhead.}\n\\label{sec:runtime}\nS1 overhead is $+$13.1\\% on SciERC and $+$2.43\\% on DocRED (amortized over longer sequences); under LoRA, $+$10.0\\% with 37\\% lower absolute latency.\nGrammar compile: one-time 44\\,ms (Appendix~\\ref{app:runtime}).\n\n\\paragraph{Beam search compatibility.}\n\\label{sec:beam-results}\nBeam search ($k{=}4$) with LoRA eliminates all residual violations on SciERC for both Qwen and Llama (R-F1 44.23, 46.11).\nUnder zero-shot, beam search reduces DR to 0\\% for calibrated models and CF substantially (9.90\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.53\\% Qwen; 6.56\\%\\,$\\to$\\,0.93\\% Llama; Appendix~\\ref{app:beam-search}).\n\n% exp_ids: 70b_scierc_unconstrained, 70b_scierc_constrained, 70b_docred_unconstrained, 70b_docred_constrained\n\\paragraph{Cross-scale generalizability.}\n\\label{sec:70b-results}\nOn Llama-3.1-70B (AWQ INT4), OntoCoDec achieves DR$\\to$0\\% on SciERC (R-F1 $-$0.03\\,pp) and \\textbf{0\\%} across ET, RV, and DR on the full DocRED 998-document set (R-F1 $+$0.04\\,pp), the only configuration to completely eliminate DR on DocRED.\nCF rises to 7.01\\% (from 0.07\\%); the low baseline reflects under-measurement (malformed outputs bypass the checker), while constrained decoding exposes more triples to the consistency check (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\nModel scale reduces but does not eliminate violations."
